It is good stock, cut small, and written in a fine old hand: your name, correctly spelled, and beneath it an hour, and beneath that the word Corvane. There is no date upon it. The hand has written a great many of these.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You put the card in the inside of your coat, where it lies flat against the ribs, and go on with the day's work. It is an invitation and not a summons, which means the invitation is the point, and you will keep it until you are the one who chooses the hour.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You set the card beside the abbey's register and the two hands are one hand, three hundred years apart, with the same broken loop on the same letter. He wrote his own name into the parish book as a boy and he wrote this card last week, and he has not learned a new letter in either. You go on knowing something true about him, which in this valley is a rare kind of ammunition.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You hold the card to the light and the hand is old and fine and gives you nothing you can use. You put it back inside your coat and go on with the day.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The bell in the rubble
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The abbey bell lies in the rubble where the tower left it, cracked at the lip and green with three hundred years of weather, and it has just rung, with the rubble undisturbed and nobody within fifty yards of it. The frost moves it, the villagers say, when they will say anything at all, and today the ground is soft and the air is mild.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You note the hour in your chronicle and pass on up the orchard rows. The bell rings twice more before you are out of the ground, at the same interval both times, which is the interval a sexton keeps.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:sw|Count the ringing against the hour
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You sit down in the wet grass and count it out, and the bell is ringing the office. It keeps the hours the abbey kept, to the minute, three hundred years after the last brother who knew them went into the ground beneath you. Something down here has never once been told that the abbey fell, and you know now what hour it will be at prayer.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You count three ringings at three different intervals and make an office of none of them. The bell is cracked and the frost is capricious, and you go on up the rows unsatisfied.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|A man is sitting on the milestone at the head of the moor road with his pack beside him and his boots off, and he asks you, civilly and in a perfectly ordinary voice, which way is out. He has the Guild's chalk-marks copied fair on a sheet in his hand. The sheet is dated eleven years ago.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Tell him the truth and walk on
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You tell him the truth, that the road goes in and comes back in, and he thanks you and puts his boots on and starts up the road anyway, because that is the only thing there is to do. You have said the same thing to yourself a dozen times and started up the road after it.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:sw|Take his sheet and read the chalk
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You take the sheet and read the north survey's own chalk upon it, eleven years old and in a surveyor's hand you know from the Guild's own books. He walked in the season the mist closed and he has been at this milestone ever since, and he has not aged a day off the date on the sheet. You copy what the chalk is worth and give the sheet back, and he thanks you and puts his boots on.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The sheet is rain-blurred past the second waystone and gives you nothing the survey has not already got. You give it back and he thanks you and starts up the road, and you go the other way.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|A shepherd on the crag pasture is counting his fold through the gate for the third time, and getting a different number each time, and he is not a man who cannot count. Forty-one, then forty, then forty-two. He asks you to stand at the gate and count them with him, and looks relieved when you agree.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You stand at the gate and count them through with him and make it forty-one twice running, and he takes that and pens them and thanks you and does not ask what the third count was. Some arithmetic in this valley is best left at two attempts.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:sw|Count the fold by the marks on their fleeces
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You count them by the raddle-marks instead of by the head, and the sum comes out at forty every time, and the extra beast has no mark upon it at all and will not go through the gate while you are looking at the gate. The shepherd pens forty and burns a lamp in the fold all night, and both of you sleep the better for the arithmetic.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The raddle is old and half the fleeces have rubbed theirs off against the wall, and you make it forty-something and leave it there. He pens what went through and shuts the gate hard.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The tally upon the doorpost
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The tally cut into the doorpost of the Bell and Shutter runs the whole length of the post and begins again at the top, one notch a night. The potboy cuts the night's notch at dusk while the row bars its shutters, and he does it without being told and without looking up.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Let the boy cut his notch
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You stand aside and let the boy cut the night's notch, and he does it in one stroke and goes in and bars the door. Whatever the count is for, Hollowmere has kept it without missing a night for longer than anyone in the row has been alive.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You count the post from the bottom up and reckon the courses of it against the years, and the tally does not count nights at all. It counts the ones the village has sent up the crag, and the notches run a great deal thicker in some years than in others, and the thickest year is the year the mist closed.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You count two courses of the post and lose the reckoning in the third, for the cuttings overlap and the wood is old and the light is going. You leave the arithmetic and go in out of the dusk.

game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You copy the broken pillar into your chronicle beside the Undergate's figure. The warren's goblins are hauling the black stone down, by order of somebody, and the hand upon that order is steadier than any goblin's.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:sw|Follow the hauling-ropes a way
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:addBuff|The Cart-Road, Marked
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The ropes run to a block-and-tackle of dwarf salvage, and the cart-road below it is graded and swept. You mark the road upon your map: a swept road is a fast road, whoever sweeps it.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The ropes end at a sheer drop and an empty cradle where a second cart should hang. The haulers work in relays, and the relay below keeps its own counsel.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The grey watchers
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Three goblins of the old warren stand at a watch-post ahead, and they watch you pass without a thrown stone or a word. Their eyes have gone grey as tarn-water, and upon each forehead is chalked the broken pillar. The eldest raises one hand, palm out: a practised gesture, delivered like a clerk's stamp.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Return the gesture and pass
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You return the raised palm and pass. The watchers turn back to the dark below with the patience of men paid by the hour, and you feel their attention upon your back long after the post is behind you.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:sw|Speak to the eldest in trade-tongue
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:addBuff|A Watcher's Word
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The eldest answers in fair trade-tongue, unhurried: the stone dreams, the dreams pay better than the warren ever did, and the deep buys shards by the weight. It names the buyer with reverence, and the name is a title: the Speaker.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The eldest hears you out and answers with the raised palm again, precisely as before. Whatever tongue the watch-post keeps now, it is spoken further down.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The crown come down
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|A shrine of stacked black chips stands at the warren's heart, and upon it, on a cushion of folded grave-silk, sits a thing the Guild has on its books: Old Snik's crown, cast into the dark at the throne of stolen bones and come twelve floors down to be found again. The tag upon the cushion is written in a fair goblin hand: held for the queen, by the stone's leave.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Leave the crown upon its cushion
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You leave the crown where the warren has laid it and enter the shrine in your chronicle. A crown with a keeper is intelligence; a crown in your pack is a war.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:sw|Read the shrine's tally-sticks
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The tally-sticks keep the warren's new accounts: shards down, provisions up, and one column kept in sevens that never varies. Seven pieces, the sticks say, and six stand below already.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The sticks are notched in the new warren fashion, even and close, and their meaning stays with the warren. The old ragged notching was easier reading.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|From the causeway your lantern finds the paved road upon the tarn's floor, running straight and in good repair beneath two fathoms of black water. A file of shapes is walking it, far down, each bent under a load, and the water carries up no sound of them at all.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Keep to the causeway
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You keep the causeway and let the drowned road keep its traffic. Two roads run to the same place, and the dry one is yours.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:sw|Count the file and its loads
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You count the walkers against the road's milestones: bearers in relays, every one laden going down, every one empty-handed coming back. Whatever is below wants the stone faster than the stone can walk.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The water bends the lantern-light and the count breaks past twelve. The file walks on at its patient pace, far beyond your reckoning.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|You wake at your own camp with chalk-dust upon your fingers and a drawing upon the flat rock beside you, in your own hand: a standing stone, whole, upon a field you have yet to reach. The Guild's advice for the lower floors is printed on the back of every tag, and it is short: members who dream of the stone are to report the dream, and to draw no more of it.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Rub the drawing out and report it
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You rub the drawing to dust, enter the dream in your chronicle for the Guild's files, and move camp before the next watch. The advice on the tag was bought with somebody's bad night.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You study the drawing as a surveyor would, and the dream loses its grip in the measuring: the stone stands upon a field of grown rock, and the water beyond it is drawn deeper than any tarn. You know the ground now, and the knowing is yours alone.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The drawing follows you down into the next sleep, and the sleep is poor lodging. The stone stands a shade nearer in the second dream, and you wake unrested.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The duergar toll-post at the road's shoulder keeps its rates under glass, amended in a grey clerk's hand: black stone, by the weight, the price trebled and trebled again, delivery below, no questions taken or answered. A standing order at the foot of the board is older than every amendment: the seventh piece commands the buyer's own price. Name it.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Copy the rates for the Guild
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You copy the board entire for the Guild's files. A market this hungry has a bottom to it, and the bottom is paying whatever is asked; the Guild will want that figure on record before any member meets the buyer.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:sw|Haggle passage as a stone-carrier
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:addBuff|Carrier's Papers
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The toll-clerk takes you for a carrier of means, stamps a set of papers, and waves you to the swept side of the road. Carriers travel at the buyer's expense, and the buyer's credit stands beyond question.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The toll-clerk reads your hands, rules you no carrier, and returns to the ledger. The unswept side of the road serves honest traffic well enough.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Terms from under the water
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|At the field's edge the black water lies flat as a ruled line, and the voice arrives through the soles of your boots rather than the air. It addresses you by rank, correctly, as the wyrm once did, and it offers terms with a creditor's patience: leave the seventh piece where it lies, go up rich, and dream quietly ever after. The offer stands, says the voice, until the stone is whole.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You give the terms the silence they were measured for and walk on between the standing pieces. Under the water something turns a page, unhurried, and the hum of the field resumes at the old pitch.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:sw|Ask what the obelisk sealed
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The voice pauses at the question, and the pause is the answer: the stone was a seal, it says at last, and it remembers the sealing from the underside. What the elder folk pinned beneath their pillar has been speaking to you all this while.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The voice declines the subject with the smoothness of long practice and returns to the terms, of which it speaks warmly and at length.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:sw|Listen for what the voice fears
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:addBuff|The Speaker's Fear, Heard
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|Beneath the terms you hear it: the voice times its words between the far, faint strokes of the Guild bell upon the rim, and hurries the syllable that would fall upon the hour. Whatever waits under the water fears nothing you carry down to it. It fears a town that counts its own.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You listen, and hear terms, courteously put, and the flat water gives back your own lantern and nothing beneath it.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|A Guild runner picks a way to you across the silent shard-field, letter held high, boots grey to the knee with stone-dust. Item: the Speaker's fall is entered, and the survey office has ruled the obelisk a quarry, to be worked under seal. Item: the grey watchers upon the stairs have washed off their chalk marks, and the warren asks terms for honest haulage, which the Guild is minded to grant. Item: the quarry's first drills have met worked stone at depth, a lintel and a stair beneath it, and the Guild has entered the descent as Arc III: The Manifold Halls, the first chapter's terms enclosed.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The terms chart six halls below the quarry floor, from a counterfeit town to an observatory at the bottom, at about two days of descent all told. The Guild's memorandum upon retirement is enclosed also, and it is warm. The way down stands open, the sword-hook stands ready, and the choice is entered as yours.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Add a line for the warren's families

game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The visitors' book

game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Sign beneath the entry, correctly
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You sign beneath the counterfeit entry in your own hand and rule a line between the two, so that any Guild eye that follows may know which name is honest. Two entries, one arrival: the survey office will want the page entire.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:sw|Compare the strange hand with your own papers

game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The false bell rings the hour from the counterfeit Guildhouse, and seven minutes later the true bell of Mereward answers it, seventeen floors up and thin as a pin dropped down a well. The town below keeps its own time, and keeps it fast.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Enter the difference in your chronicle
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You enter the seven minutes in your chronicle for the survey office. A town that runs ahead of the true bell is keeping an appointment the true town has yet to hear of, and the Guild will want the hour of it.

game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You time the peal against your own pulse and the drip of the gate cistern: seven minutes to the second, every hour, rung from an empty belfry by a rope that hauls itself. game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The peal breaks your count at the fifth minute, twice, in the manner of a thing that knows it is being timed. You keep the cistern between yourself and the belfry for the rest of the watch.

game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The mould cut to measure

game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Terms from the eyepiece
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|At the observatory stair the nearest lens swings upon its stalk and holds you in the lens, and the master's voice arrives from all the brass at once, dry and courteous. The offer stands, says the voice, until the door at the bottom is opened.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Decline, in the Guild's correct form
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You decline in the form the Guild teaches for offers of dubious tenure, thanks first and refusal entire, and walk on beneath the turning rings. The lens follows you to the lip of the stair, and from all the brass at once the voice sends one dry word after you, that the refusal is noted.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:addBuff|The Master's Purpose, Heard
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The voice warms a degree at the question, in the way of a scholar asked after his life's work: an ending, he says. He has read ten thousand descents to their last page, and every page but the last is his own drafting, and he watches for the one caller whose ending he cannot write. The lens holds you a long moment.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|The voice takes the question, files it, and returns to the terms, of which it speaks fully and well. Somewhere above you, a pen will be setting the exchange out fair.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:addBuff|The Master's Fear, Heard
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|Beneath the courtesies you hear it: the voice pauses, every so often, in the quiet after Old Hesper's tokens ring upon the stone, and resumes a shade too smoothly. The master has charted every floor of the descent and cannot chart the counting from above: what unsettles the observatory is a town that keeps its own tally of who goes down.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You listen, and hear terms, courteously put, and under them the even turning of the rings. If the master fears a thing, it keeps to a floor you have yet to reach.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The letter at the observatory

Item: the scriptorium's pens have stopped, and the library asks any member for an account of the halls in a living hand, the fair copies being held in evidence. Item: below the last stair the Court of the Lich has adjourned between its seasons, and behind its empty dais a servants' door stands open upon offices no survey has charted; the Guild has entered the descent as Arc IV: The Roll Below, and the first floor's terms are enclosed.

game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You write the line and the runner carries it up: the collection in the menagerie is fed to the hour by no keeper the Guild employs, and the Guild's books should provide for it before the works run down. game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The Court of the Lich stands adjourned and dark, the thrones empty in their order of precedence and the dust upon the arms lying even, as though the whole court rose at once and filed out between one season and the next. The mantel above the cold hearth bears a clean ring in the dust where the jar stood through every sitting you attended, and the jar is gone from it. Behind the dais, where you have never had cause to look, a servants' door stands open upon a stair going down.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You take the servants' door behind the dais rather than wait upon a sitting that will not come, and start down the stair after the jar. The court has been a door all along, and no adventurer before you has passed behind the dais.

Something called the whole court down between seasons and left the door open behind it. You go down the better for what the order of the empty court has told you.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You count the thrones and their precedence and make what you can of the even dust, and in the end the cold room keeps its own counsel. You take the open door regardless, for the jar went down it.

The earliest volumes are written in the elder script of the Undergate arch, and the lead of their bindings is untarnished. You have seen this manifest before, in pieces, all the way down.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Copy a column for the survey and pass

game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:sw|Match the struck names against your own descent
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:addBuff|The Manifest, Read
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You match the struck names against the road behind you, and the account closes: every warden entered at the head of a season and struck through at its foot is a capstone you have put down, one to a stratum, all the way from the sealed door to the halls. The things that climb the Maw are debts leaking upward through a failing seal, and every capstone slain has drawn a pin from the lock. You go down knowing at last what the descent has been.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You read a volume or two and make out that the names are struck in the order they fell, but the hand is close and the elder script closer, and the whole of it will want a clerk and a lamp and a quiet week. You take a tracing and go on.

Six of the seven you read as capstones the descent has already put down. An eighth tomb is begun in the far wall, the outline chalked and the first course cut, and the chalk gives no name.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You copy the seven names from the brass into your chronicle, and beside six of them you enter the floor upon which you put that warden down. The survey keeps a file upon the wardens, and the file has wanted these names since the first sealed door.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:sw|Measure yourself against the eighth outline

Seven wardens sealed the Maw, one to a stratum, and six of them lie slain behind you; the seventh keeps his court still, and the eighth grave waits upon the one who unseals the last door. You step out of the outline and go down.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You pace the chalked outline and make it a tall grave cut for no tenant the gallery names yet, and leave the measuring at that. The mason's tools are warm, and you keep that to yourself and go on.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The seal, thinnest here
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The gallery of cells runs on into the cold, a great chain gathered at each door into a lock, and every lock filed patiently through from the inside. The terms posted upon the doors run to figures no debtor could serve in ten lifetimes, and the deepest cells stand broken outward, the doors sprung by a shoulder set against them from within. The seal is thinnest in this deep, and its weeping stands cold upon the walls and runs downward, while the air of the place moves upward past you, drawn toward the empty court like a draught toward an open flue.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|Note the broken cells and keep moving
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You enter the sprung doors and the filed locks in your chronicle and keep to the centre of the gallery, out of reach of the open cells. Whatever served its term here served it out from the inside, and went upward, and the Guild will want the count of the empty doors.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:addBuff|The Leak, Read
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You read the deep by its draught and its water: the seal leaks upward here, and what it lets loose climbs cell by cell and floor by floor toward the surface and the Guild's own noticeboard rather than falling to the Bottom. Everything that ever came up the Maw came up this way, through a wound the wardens have been driving shut since before Mereward. You go down toward the driving.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You feel the cold draught climbing past you and the water running the other way, and make of it that the deep breathes wrong, and leave the reading there. The gallery is no place to stand and think, and you go on.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The drum you have heard since the goblin warren beats here at last, and it is a counterweight upon a beam, keeping the count of the works in sevens. The seal weeps, and the works return the weeping to the wound: pumps the height of a house draw the gathered water up and drive it back against the seal, and the water you have waded upon every floor above begins in this room. Far below the beam's fall, a beat late, a deeper sound answers, and the needles upon the gauges lean the merest hair at the answering.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You enter the working of the sealing-works in your chronicle for the survey: what gathers the water, what drives it back, and the count kept in sevens the whole while. game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You time the drum against the sound that answers it from below, and the count comes clear: the works keep the water off a wound, and the thing beyond the wound answers the beam a beat late, patient as a debtor turning in its sleep. game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:log|You count the drum's sevens and hear the deeper beat answer late, and make of it that the deep keeps a bad rhythm, and leave the timing there. The works are loud and the floor is wet, and you go on.
game/lowmoor.js|1|property:fire|call:jf|The servants' stair ends in the Last Vault, and the vault is furnished as a study and kept as a fortress: shelves of an age of working-books, a great desk with the enrolling-pen laid square upon the blank first page of a new Roll, and the seven seals of the wardens hung upon the wall, six struck through in lead and the seventh whole. Upon the mantel stands the jar, plain clay, dusted and centred, with the fire laid ready beneath it and never lit. At the desk a figure sits with its back to you, and it speaks without turning, and it says that it has kept your chair as it has kept the count, and that you are expected.

The jar is the lock upon the last door, and the door stands open at your back. Set the lock anew and take up the count, and the deep will hold another age. Or go down, and settle the debt beneath by your own hand, and let the count end with you. The choice was never offered to me. The mill's day-book lies open upon the bin, and the last entry in it is a note of the day's grinding, and the hand is steady, and the date is three hundred years old.

It carries no clock, and it will keep in your satchel until you answer it. It reads: "To whoever has come up my crag, and there have been a number. You will have been told in the village that I keep this valley. It is truer to say that the valley and I were shut in together, and that I have been the only one of us able to do anything about it. Four things may be done in my hall tonight and I have thought about all four for three hundred years. Burn me and the Vale keeps its mist and loses its lord, which is the answer the village would give if the village were asked. Bring up what the abbot's chapel was built to hold and let the morning in, if you have found it and if you can carry it. Take the ring off my hand and the seat at my table and keep the Vale as it must be kept, if you have learned that the house keeps to a hand and not to a man. Or read out the name I was christened before ever there was a count here, if the abbey wrote it down and you have read it. I commend the choosing to you. I have had no one to commend it to before."
